## 2026-11-06 PT-TIMEOUT-1: live progress and execution diagnostics

Implemented [SPEC-01a](specs/ghidriff-timeout-progress.md) on `fix/ghidriff-timeout-progress-20261106`, based on `d8cfd7b1d8bffa90af1c7af94dda012b89886b76`. Process observations are bounded to 16 KiB per captured stream, delivered on the supervisor thread outside the reader lock at most once per second plus a final snapshot. The UI shows the effective subprocess budget, elapsed time, last observed stage and output age with indeterminate progress. Heartbeats do not fill the log; cancellation, terminal text and exact-worker identity take precedence. Timeout errors include the Setup route and project-relative diagnostics directory. Atomic best-effort `backend-execution.json` receipts record process outcome and available safe runtime identifiers without claiming normalized comparison success or inventing unprobed versions. No matching, argv, timeout-default, cache-key or threading changes.

Regression evidence: the existing UI elapsed timer overwrote terminal messages while the worker was still active. Independent review also reproduced misclassification of the supplied `Starting Ghidra analysis of ...` sequence and acceptance of credential-shaped single-token version metadata. Those cases now have passing regression coverage. Review corrected two timing-test assumptions by synchronizing the child's quiet interval with the observer and avoiding an invalid child-sleep lower bound on the narrower supervisor clock.

Focused verification: **13 supervisor tests passed**, including real authored children, quiet heartbeats, fixed deadlines despite output, callback failures, bounded separate streams, cancellation/descendant cleanup, output flooding and cleanup-excluded duration. **41 backend diagnostics tests passed**, including a real authored child that cannot finish until progress is observed, receipt-write failures, version redaction, cache/identical skips and prior-result preservation. **11 offscreen Qt tests passed** for live main-thread delivery, no heartbeat log growth, indeterminate percentages, stale-event rejection, normalization/reset and terminal/cancel priority. An earlier combined integration run passed **255 tests with 3 skips** before the two pure-parser review fixes. These are synthetic process/UI controls, not live Ghidriff compatibility or performance evidence.

Full local integration run: **18,705 passed, 3 failed, 134 skipped, 7 xfailed, 1 warning and 3 teardown errors in 2,333.40 seconds**. Before this completed run, the editable install was rebound to this checkout and verified with an isolated `python -I` import; earlier stopped attempts are not counted. The failures identified the existing `ui.py` size ceiling, a GZF regression assertion that did not allow the new observational events, and the environment's `AF_UNIX` restriction. The `ui.py` relay was extracted into `ui_job_events.py`, retaining the existing 3,077-line ceiling (current file: 3,076); the GZF test now preserves its original core lifecycle/settings assertions while allowing progress/stage observations. The affected host-seam/GZF suites pass **49 tests**.

The three new Qt fixture teardown errors were reproduced independently and traced to pytest-qt scheduling the window for deletion before yield-fixture finalizers. Instrumentation confirmed the worker stopped, then the window was destroyed before its queued `job_finished` callback could clear the Python worker reference. Cleanup now uses the supported `before_close_func` hook, joins the actual worker within the unchanged 5,000 ms bound, dispatches completion while the window is still valid, and asserts the normal callback cleared `window.worker`; it never clears that state manually. All test bodies remain unchanged. Three repeated collections in one process passed **33 tests in 31.47 seconds**.

The Unix-socket failure is independently reproduced by isolated stdlib `socket.socket(socket.AF_UNIX)` with **no PatchTrace module imported**, raising the same `PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted` before the network guard runs. The test is retained without changing environment access controls. The only warning is the intentional duplicate-ZIP-entry test; native-tool/platform acceptance remains separately blocked or skipped as reported by the suite. This local full run is not green and is not claimed as final-head hosted acceptance.

Initial corrected-tree combined regression run: **315 passed, 3 skipped in 69.69 seconds**, covering supervisor/progress, backend diagnostics, existing jobs/UI/bundle setup, host-seam rules, GZF retention, spec registry and tracker integrity. Whole-tree Ruff and `git diff --check` pass. Schema regeneration produced no changes to the existing public schemas. Independent re-review approved the production changes, event assertions and fixture lifecycle repair.

PR #251 automated-review follow-up: use explicit `stage not yet observed` until a marker is recognized; preserve the known `unsafe-output-path` receipt reason; emit `analysis-stopped` immediately after supervision returns/raises and before diagnostics/cleanup/validation, letting the ordinary desktop job clock continue; omit only `analysis-progress` heartbeats from both CLI listeners. Seven backend regression cases and seven CLI cases first failed before repair. The backend suite then passed **48 tests**, the CLI/series group passed **61**, and the updated UI/host-seam group passed **36**. CLI cases simulate 7,200 heartbeats per scenario while checking exact retained events, real normalization/persistence and JSON exit envelopes without sleeping or running an external engine.

Combined regression run after the four review follow-ups at `a908f90`: **362 passed, 3 skipped in 71.78 seconds**. Whole-tree Ruff and whitespace checks pass, and independent review found no remaining blocker in those four fixes. The existing core lifecycle assertions still reject unexpected events while allowing the explicitly specified observational events.

A further review identified that infrequent CLI stage messages still wrote to stderr synchronously during process supervision. Four production-wiring regressions reproduced attempted stderr writes during real-child timeout/cancellation in both series and selftest. A per-comparison adapter now suppresses heartbeats and retains at most the latest 64 stage observations without I/O; it flushes them in order, with an explicit omission count after overflow, when supervision ends. Desktop signals remain live. This preserves process deadline/cancellation checks even when the CLI writer is blocked; it does not promise non-blocking terminal output after process stop.

Receipt persistence now flushes and syncs the temporary file before atomic replacement. Three injected sync-failure cases failed before repair; afterward, **51 backend diagnostics tests passed in 2.55 seconds**, including unchanged analysis outcome, preservation of an earlier receipt on final-write failure, temporary-file removal and fixed warning text. This file-only sync is best effort, without a full filesystem/power-loss guarantee. Independent review found no blocker in the receipt follow-up.

Final combined regression run including the CLI stage-deferral and receipt-sync follow-up: **377 passed, 3 skipped in 176.28 seconds**. This includes four real-child blocked-writer cases, bounded backlog/omission ordering and fresh-adapter isolation, along with the earlier backend/UI/CLI and integration checks. Cancellation cases allow five seconds for slower Windows startup; timeout cases retain their shorter fixed budget. Whole-tree Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Independent final review found no blocker in the adapter, both production call sites, tests or amended contract.

Hosted full-suite follow-up at `d4255840c56eebfb168d9506ff49e9d181dc8a9f`: [Linux QA](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42232172111/job/127941286463) completed with **18,824 passed, 1 failed, 47 skipped, 7 xfailed and 1 warning in 1,629.36 seconds**. The sole failure was `test_cli_registry.py::test_cli_seam_budget`: the cumulative CLI callback changes grew `cli.py` by nine lines against the unchanged six-line allowance. The earlier focused group did not include this separate CLI registry gate. No runtime test failed in this hosted Linux run; its lint/package steps did not execute after pytest failed.

The CLI seam repair replaces the nested stderr callback and its redundant heartbeat filter with a two-line fresh `CliBackendProgress` listener assignment. The adapter already owns heartbeat filtering and stage deferral; formatting, event ordering, normalization, persistence and exit behavior remain unchanged. This naturally reduces file growth from nine to six lines, meeting the existing gate without changing its cap. A focused rerun including CLI progress, registry, backend CLI, series and headless-entry tests passed **129 tests with 1 skip in 15.87 seconds**. Whole-tree Ruff and whitespace checks pass, and independent review approved the repair and unchanged gate. Hosted acceptance is still required on the resulting repair head.

Hosted acceptance at `3b488913b177dd63a6a42c8eef51a03fe2526824` confirmed the CLI gate repair: [full Linux QA](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42237741377/job/127960075756) passed **18,825 tests, with 47 skips, 7 xfails and 1 warning**, followed by clean Ruff. All GUI repeat gates passed: **37,740 test executions across four Linux shards with 20 repetitions**, and **9,435 executions with five Windows repetitions**. [Native Windows Ghidriff acceptance](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42237741478) and [unified extraction on both platforms](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42237741832) also passed for this head.

[Full Windows QA](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42237741377/job/127960076037) at `3b488913` reported **18,788 passed, 4 failed, 81 skipped, 6 xfailed and 1 warning in 2,816.50 seconds**. All four failures were new `test_jobs_progress.py` assertions: three expected LF-only print output and a hard-coded byte count although Windows preserved CRLF; the cleanup-duration test compared its requested 0.4-second sleep against separately sampled clock intervals, which produced a 0.391-second gap. The preceding process/observer/exit assertions passed. The trace does not establish a production timing defect. Windows lint, build and package smoke steps were skipped after pytest failed, so this run did not produce an accepted Windows distributable.

The Windows portability repair changes only those regression assertions: derive native print output from `os.linesep`, compare exact text and raw bytes without normalization, and derive byte totals from the expected bytes. The raw-stream UTF-8 boundary fixture remains unchanged. The cleanup test measures the injected interval on the same monotonic clock, retains a 0.3-second minimum induced delay, and allows only `1e-6` for subtraction precision when comparing wall-minus-execution time with that measured interval. Final snapshot/result equality is retained. The affected jobs-progress, jobs, CLI-progress and backend-progress group passed **134 tests with 3 existing skips in 23.19 seconds** locally; whole-tree Ruff and whitespace checks pass. Independent review approved the test-only correction. No production code or skips changed; fresh Windows and package acceptance remains required.

Initial PR head `9e554dba2bcdfa7b9e080c3521cdf6b647eff2f3` passed all five [native Windows Ghidriff fixture jobs](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42226182868) and [unified extraction on Windows and Linux](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42226182885). These are fixture results for that specific head; the review follow-up requires fresh hosted checks before merge.

Intermediate head `a908f90c3066999db8bb9b7e03de192307acb1ec` also passed all five [native Windows jobs](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42228604908) and [unified extraction on Windows and Linux](https://github.com/djshellshoxxx/patchtrace/actions/runs/42228604838). These results precede the CLI stage-deferral/receipt-sync follow-up and cannot qualify its final head.

Replaying the supplied log through the fixed-stage observer identifies candidate processing at line 419 and enhanced-symbol processing at line 420, without promoting the final percentage to comparison completion; no owner binary was rerun. Hosted full-suite, Windows/package and native-backend gates on the corrected PR head remain required. The high-priority timeout incident and exact owner input-pair rerun remain **OPEN**; a longer budget or these diagnostics do not demonstrate repair of the structural-candidate slow path.

## 2026-11-02 PR #153 advisory review follow-up

At PR head `0cf22c9520da6d2841d27c0aa8136f36657aecc6`, review found that every single native snapshot without a zoned date was labelled order-ambiguous, and that multiple unorderable snapshots could leave an edge from only one snapshot presented as current. A single snapshot no longer warns. When ambiguous same-month snapshots disagree on KB/CVE/product applicability, discrepant rows are retained as disputed with deterministic conflict IDs and a `REVISION_EDGE_CONFLICT` warning; they are not called current or superseded. The MSRC specification records this limit. No revision sequence is inferred from unverified values.

Other review controls: missing native fields resolve diagnostics to an existing parent pointer; the offline XML namespace audit now constructs prefix-to-URI pairs; context construction tries a JSON-locatable CSAF card when a preferred CVRF/XML card cannot be bound and explicitly reports when no card can be sourced. Added regressions cover these cases and preserve raw source dependencies.

Validation on the local PR #153 tree: `PYTHONPATH=.:src:tests QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen python -m pytest -q tests/test_kbc_*.py` passed **1,054 tests**; whole-tree Ruff and `git diff --check` passed. The first focused run had transient failures because existing tests still expected disagreeing snapshots to remain current; the updated behavior and regressions pass in the final KB/CVE run. Current-head hosted checks and independent re-review are pending; this does not qualify the PR for merge or release.

## Repaired audit defects
- Ghidra rejected `runs/.quarantine-<uuid>/raw` with `Path element starting with '.' is not permitted`. Active and normalization-failed run directories now use `quarantine-<uuid>`. Preflight validates every resolved output ancestor, with an actionable error for dot-prefixed names and excessively long Windows output paths. The fake backend reproduces this exact rejection; native Ghidriff confirmation on Windows and clean-user reinstall remain release checks.
- Artifact storage could escape project roots: pure models and disk paths now reject traversal, absolute/drive paths and symlinks.
- Same-run evidence could be cited against the wrong function match: exact match/function and explicit neighbor associations are enforced.
- Repeated content-addressed contexts could conflict with global uniqueness: identical context/evidence can be shared; conflicting identities rejected.
- Backend module imports could be shadowed by project-local modules: configured trusted runtime cwd and removal of PYTHONPATH/PYTHONHOME.
- Windows process descendants require containment before running: suspended launch, Job Object assignment, kill-on-close then resume; failure terminates suspended child.
- Identical analysis result and failed runs needed separate transactional persistence rather than treating a directory as completed JSON.
- SQLite connections must remain on their creating thread; background project creation closes its connection before GUI reopens.
- Split/merged code view line offsets must map exact evidence lines rather than equal addresses/line numbers.
- Dynamic imported labels use plain text rather than automatic rich text.
- Large import parsing/normalization moved behind prepare/apply worker seam; atomic project changes on GUI thread.
- Ghidra/Python/pyghidra versions and dependency diagnostics are recorded, with unverified Java compatibility visible.
- Missing persisted instruction arrays are displayed as unavailable, never fabricated assembly.

- Unchanged Windows inputs were incorrectly rejected because path and open-handle ctime semantics differ: compare each source consistently, with native ChangeTime protecting against hidden edits.
- Large repetitive code could spend excessive time in line matching: bounded strategies and a disclosed linear fallback now cap the expensive path.
- Failed normalization after native execution needed quarantine and a persisted failed attempt; late cancellation cannot promote output.
- Export preview and actual bytes needed identical option handling and deterministic rendering.
- Optional Qt image/input plugins pulled unused PDF and virtual-keyboard dependencies into the freeze: custom desktop plugin collection now prevents those imports, with an executable-component rejection gate and post-build ZIP inspection.
- Qt wheels lacked redistributed license texts: exact upstream notice sources are vendored; the builder refuses missing required notices and excluded WebEngine/PDF collection.

- Native Ghidra workspace isolation: new regression tests require Ghidriff to receive an external private `--project-location` and `--gzfs-path`, check cleanup on success/failure, and preserve case-owned JSON/logs. Temporary engine workspaces should not be inside synced project directories; new Windows native compatibility must be rerun after this change.

- Ghidriff symbol-server behavior is now opt-in via an explicit saved GUI setting. The backend passes `--no-symbols` by default and records the choice in run provenance. Tests exercise both modes and reject ambiguous types. A passing no-symbols CLI fixture is not proof of OS-enforced network isolation.

- Local runtime discovery is a separate, read-only stdlib module with bounded direct-child scanning and no process execution. Setup prefills only unique existing candidates and preserves analyst-selected paths. This reduces manual path navigation but does not install or validate the full runtime; automated fixture coverage is distinct from real Windows/OneDrive acceptance.

- Native backend failure classifier maps supported error signatures to fixed actionable guidance (JDK mismatch, missing Ghidriff, Ghidra path, heap/disk/permissions), preserving original exit code and sanitized log. Unknown signatures retain the generic inspect-analysis.log message. Regression tests assert no tokens from raw upstream output are echoed.

- Offline bundle importer: strict versioned manifest/ZIP inventory SHA-256 checks, Windows path/type checks, per-user atomic staging/lock with cancellation/rollback, no network or payload execution in the install path, dedicated child-only Java environment. Synthetic payload fixtures verify importer plumbing, not a functional relocatable Ghidriff distribution. Spec-06 full disconnected Windows bundle acceptance remains pending.

## 2026-10-12 delivery session

- Cached-PDB `FileExistsError` under `--no-symbols` (PR #9): root cause traced in pinned Ghidriff `setup_project()` (skips `setup_symbol_server` then calls `get_pdb(allow_remote=False)`, which consults the persisted SymbolServerService) and Ghidra 12.0.4 `ApplicationUtilities.getDefaultUserSettingsDir` (`application.settingsdir`, then `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` on every OS). Fix: per-run `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` in private scratch. Native Windows CI matrix: plain Ghidriff with a persisted store **reproduces** the failure; PatchTrace analysis **passes** with the persisted store (with and without PDBs at the embedded path) and clean. The user's clfs.sys pair and Ghidra 12.1.4 were not available; the fixture is the compiled bounds-check DLL. A CI fixture regression (MSVC `/DEBUG` implying `/INCREMENTAL` thunks) was fixed with `/INCREMENTAL:NO`.
- Linux x86-64 onedir package: CI smoke on Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 offscreen and Xvfb/xcb (initial failure: missing Qt xcb libraries on runners; full list now installed and documented). Frozen Linux binary runs `rank` end to end locally.
- Offline installer (PR #7) security review: 8 defects fixed with regression tests (non-normalized/system install roots, junctions, PT-CANCEL, cleanup masking, zlib errors, extended device names, POSIX replace race, plan contract). Real relocatable runtime payload and air-gapped Windows acceptance remain open.
- Phase 2 enrichment (SPEC-07, PatchTrace-authored; supplied handoff schema/examples absent): B1 detectors, B2 WIL, B3 CVE normalization/transport/contextual ranking, B4 cache, additive storage, pipeline, CLI, desktop and report wiring. Independent review found 6 defects, all fixed with reproducing tests: cancelled stages cached as empty results (scores later dropped to 0); non-default CLI profiles shown as the default in GUI/export; cache hits not revalidated against live ownership; file-loaded CVE snapshots trusted for feature IDs and stale snapshots unreported; non-object weights traceback; stale rows recording the live revision. Storage tests also caught non-atomic DDL in Python's legacy sqlite3 mode (fixed with explicit `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`). Benchmark found the WIL stage never hit the cache when empty (fixed).
- CVE endpoints probed live once by the B3 implementer (2026-10-12): NVD CVE API 2.0 and MSRC `sug/v2.0` per-CVE; fixtures are trimmed real responses for CVE-2024-38063. No live fetch runs in tests. Model/transport success does not validate CVE relevance.
- Benchmark (`tools/bench_enrichment.py`, synthetic 10,000 pairs / 110,000 lines, 4 vCPU Linux container, Python 3.12.3): cold pipeline 6.6 s and 240 MiB peak RSS in a fresh process (objectives 10 s / 512 MiB met on this machine); derived-cache hit 3.1 s, **objective 500 ms not met** (dominated by input hashing and ownership revalidation, which are required for correctness). UI responsiveness at this scale not measured. Windows timings not measured.

- Release `v0.1.0-beta.80` (prerelease) built by CI from merge commit `0d22e9c` (PR #10), tag verified to point at that commit. Downloaded assets: `PatchTrace-0.1.0-windows-x64.zip` 47,682,397 bytes and `PatchTrace-0.1.0-linux-x86_64.tar.gz` 74,017,268 bytes; both `.sha256` sidecars verified locally. Extracted Linux artifact in a clean `env -i`: `--self-test` exit 0, `rank` on the synthetic sample returns `read_item` (score 26), `flags` exit 4 (no results), missing project exit 2. Windows ZIP: 452 entries, contains `PatchTrace.exe`, enrichment schemas and updated README; the Windows frozen self-test and `rank --help` CLI smoke ran in CI `qa (windows-latest)`. No hands-on Windows GUI session was performed.

Retained with unmerged work: `claude/patchtrace-cli-dotpath-spec-er6hha` (SPEC-05 CLI/config/build-provenance draft; its exit-code table conflicts with the Phase 2 contract and its config/buildinfo modules are not integrated).

## 2026-10-12 full code audit (5 areas) and repairs

Every fix has a test that fails before and passes after. Confirmed defects fixed:
- Runtime (A): URL credential redaction leaked partial passwords and missed non-HTTP schemes; backend probe failed whenever the interpreter wrote a stderr warning (wrong "install ghidriff" diagnosis); Ghidra discovery could miss installs in crowded folders; a scratch-cleanup failure turned a completed analysis into a failure (now bounded retries + warning); post-kill wait timeouts and output-inspection OSErrors escaped without failure.txt; quoted secret values and bare GitHub/AWS/Bearer tokens were not redacted.
- Core (B): project database creation was not atomic (failed create left an unusable folder); strict JSON accepted overflowing floats and raised untyped errors on huge integers; save accepted a foreign project or lower revision; public annotate could forge match-override records; failed-backup cleanup could fail on read-only files; backup publish had a POSIX replace race; service.project went stale after backup recovered interrupted runs.
- AI/reports (C): provider requests had no total deadline (slow-drip servers held them open, ignoring Cancel); Windows paths with spaces leaked usernames into reports; Markdown ranking cells could be broken by line breaks.
- Desktop (D): untrusted bundle names rendered as rich text in the install consent prompt; tooltips fed binary names, model summaries and signal rationales could render rich text; empty export destination crashed on format change; failed self-test leaked the project lock; corrupt numeric settings crashed window construction.
- Enrichment/CLI (E): BND missed Ghidra cast pointer arithmetic and `else if` guards; SIZE fired on decompiler temporary spills; WIL qualified whitespace/rename-only edits and treated string/comment text as sites; WIL qualifying-hunk recomputation was quadratic (121 s on a 4,100-site function, now ~1 s); cancelled correlate and Ctrl-C in JSON mode broke the exit/envelope contract; zero-pairing rank could never exit 4; misleading stale note for non-default profiles; quarantine could move a just-republished valid cache entry; leased entries could be evicted when the lease file failed; prune trash was unreclaimable; one failed or malformed provider response discarded the others; any CWE dispute blocked agreed CWEs; duplicate snapshots double-counted; cancelled rankings were published.
- CLI/GUI/export agreement for the default ranking: verified identical (rank, match, score, raw, evidence, status, config digest).

Documented hypotheses left open (not reproduced): POSIX killpg PID-reuse race, setsid descendants holding pipes, Windows environment block sort order, diagnostic phrase false positives, Ghidra-directory sys.path shadowing, duplicate upstream addresses in Ghidriff JSON, ignored extra modified-entry keys, undo limited to the latest correction, off-thread `prepare_*` reads of service state (safe while mutating controls are disabled), export overwrite without confirmation for typed paths, positional project-directory argument (unspecified). Windows-only confirmations pending: tooltip/QMessageBox rendering, read-only backup cleanup, scratch retry sufficiency, pid liveness and junction checks in the cache.

Phase 3a acquisition (SPEC-08, headless): Winbindex parsing, consented msdl download with blob-only redirect, quarantine and PE/PA30 identification, CLI `acquire search|fetch`. Manual live probe: afd.sys `D37888A1b2000` downloaded through the transport, 744,832 bytes, SHA-256 matched Winbindex. `delta.py` (msdelta ctypes) has not run on Windows. Desktop dialog not yet built.

- Phase 2 completion (PR #12): profiles and raw-output cache; native Windows CI `raw-cache-hit` ran real Ghidra 12.0.4 / Temurin 25 twice and verified the second run was a cache hit with byte-identical raw JSON and identical matches.
- Phase 3 (PR #13): N-way series, acquisition dialog, BinDiff/rz-diff secondary engines. Windows CI ran the msdelta `CreateDeltaB` → `ApplyDeltaB` round trip successfully. Two Windows-only test defects found by per-test CI jobs and fixed: a 64 MiB pytest parameter id stalled the Windows console (QA hung 18+ minutes), and a POSIX path literal in an rz-diff probe test. QA jobs are now capped at 30 minutes.
- Release `v0.1.0-beta.90` built from `7eea001` (tag verified): Windows ZIP 47,861,849 bytes, Linux tar.gz 74,215,035 bytes, both sidecar checksums verified after download; extracted Linux artifact passes `--self-test` and every headless command answers `--help` with exit 0. No live BinDiff/rizin run, no hands-on Windows GUI session.

## 2026-10-13 SPEC-26 alpha session (Claude Code)

Merged: #19 (recorded file overview), #20 (handoff docs), #21 (verified artifact byte seam), #22 (PE overview + Authenticode probe), #23 (Hex/Binary/Base64 providers), #24 (tooltip registry). Each merged only after its own green GitHub Actions run: Ubuntu and Windows pytest + Ruff, Windows onedir freeze, Linux package + 22.04/24.04 smoke, and the native Windows Ghidriff matrix.

Automated evidence (actual results):
- Baseline main `f0086f7` before work: Linux local 774 passed / 5 skipped, Ruff clean.
- PR #22 Windows `qa (windows-latest)`: 875 passed / 10 skipped. JUnit shows `test_native_unsigned_file_does_not_raise` **passed** on the Windows runner, i.e. a real `WinVerifyTrust` call on an unsigned temp file returned without error. This is not acceptance of signed/trusted/revoked samples.
- Integration branch (PE comparison in File overview, raw views, Authenticode row): Linux local 973 passed / 6 skipped, Ruff clean. Windows result: see the integration PR checks.

Release publication (handoff B1) root cause: run 38492874312 (commit `64fda061`) built and tested successfully but `softprops/action-gh-release` got HTTP 403 `Resource not accessible by integration` although the job token had `contents: write`. That commit added `.github/workflows/import-agent-skills-once.yml`. GitHub refuses to let `GITHUB_TOKEN` create refs/tags on a commit whose diff adds or changes workflow files (the token can never hold the `workflows` permission). The next main runs 101 and 103 published `v0.1.0-beta.101` and `v0.1.0-beta.103` normally, and the repository has no rulesets. Conclusion: not a standing permission problem; no settings change required. Expect the same 403 on any future main push whose head commit edits `.github/workflows/*`; that run's ZIP stays available as an Actions artifact. A maintainer PAT with `workflow` scope would avoid it but is not recommended for an automated job.

Not performed in this session: interactive clean-Windows GUI walk-through, DPI/theme checks, signed/trusted/revoked Authenticode samples, fuzzing beyond the synthetic hostile-PE fixtures, live Ghidriff on new inputs beyond the CI fixture matrix.

Python 3.12.10, Ghidra 12.0.4, Temurin Java 25; pinned Ghidriff revision from research. Both MSVC-built DLLs were statically analyzed, never executed. One modified change record contains the expected check. This single fixture does not establish general matching accuracy.
- Live Ollama and remote provider paths: unverified; no configured model/credentials supplied. Mocked HTTP request/envelope/schema tests are present. Follow SPEC-03 acceptance procedures; successful transport does not validate model truth.

- Matching precision/recall, held-out real patch corpus and resource targets: unmeasured. No accuracy or speed benchmark claimed.
- Dependency archive audit: CI ZIP downloaded and inspected; SHA-256 matched, all listed files existed, 47 Core/GUI attribution pages and required notice texts were present; two Qt Network attribution pages were subsequently added for the linked Network DLL. Curated package: 46,370,158 bytes, 417 ZIP entries; SHA-256 `c5783e25146029a741a5450209638a15cf9f955e6e24582c04277325079e95b2`. Qt Core/Gui/Network/Widgets and Windows/offscreen/style/network/TLS plugins remain. No PDF/WebEngine/QML/Quick/virtual-keyboard executable components. Final release is rebuilt from the merged commit and has its own accompanying checksum.

## Practical limits
Provider cancellation is cooperative around network reads; an active read can wait until the 120-second timeout, and transmitted remote context cannot be recalled. Native decompiler crashes/timeouts preserve logs and quarantined output; process isolation is not an OS sandbox. Backend output contracts are pinned to inspected Ghidriff; unknown output fields fail clearly. Only static PE AMD64 is initially supported. Call graph visualization, runtime debugging, automatic reproduction, auto-download and additional formats are deferred. The synthetic sample is authored and explicitly labeled.

## 2026-10-14 rz-diff stream separation
`jobs.run_process(separate_streams=True)` drains stdout and stderr on independent bounded threads (Windows: second inheritable pipe, Job Object containment unchanged). rz-diff parses stdout only and reports stderr on non-zero exit. Local Linux: 981 passed, 6 skipped; Ruff clean. Windows pipe path and real rizin remain unverified until CI/live runs.

## Second audit fixes (2026-10-14, items 3-13; local Linux evidence, CI pending)
- `tests/test_audit2_fixes.py` plus one case in `tests/test_ui_setup_dialog.py`. Restricted child env keeps inherited `JAVA_HOME`/`JDK_HOME` (ghidra only, when no private JDK is configured); `env_passthrough` now rejects `PYTHON*`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`.
- Stored default/profile pipeline rows are served only when every recorded stage version equals `STAGE_VERSIONS` (a v1 row after the v2 bump reads as stale).
- `extension_version` 2 is understood; stores are still created at 1 and stamped 2 only when the first `engine-run` or `secondary/2` row is written, so v1 clients keep full access to projects that never used SPEC-28. `load_secondary` skips an invalid or hash-corrupt row with a warning; `cache_snapshot` treats a corrupt engine-run row as a miss.
- `engine-run` rows are pruned to the newest 8 per engine/pair after each run (rows tied to a stored set are kept) and are the first thing the 128 MiB cap fallback evicts; `secondary-matches` is never evicted. `secondary run` records a storage failure (row over 32 MiB, cap) as a failed EngineRun plus warning for that engine only (exit 3); `PT-SECONDARY-INPUT` from engine output is exit 3.
- Rank: `pipeline.prepare_publish` makes the verified pre-enrichment/pre-retention backup on the worker thread (own read-only connection, `.part` then rename); the owner-thread backup is skipped only when a backup of the identical snapshot exists, otherwise it still runs. Not done: the publish itself still runs on the GUI thread (it must use the owner connection); an empty NVD key variable is now no key.

Named acceptance tests present: the 06 s9 list except the two Diaphora tests (DA-01 adapter, not S0b) and `test_bindiff_algorithm_tier_map_and_basicblocks_zero`, whose stub-row half is impossible (A-16 gap, 02 s10 item 8a; the tier map and the recorded limitation are tested as `test_bindiff_algorithm_tier_map_and_stub_rows_are_a_recorded_limitation`), the 05 s1/s2.6 tests, `test_same_class_never_corroborates_across_components`, G-U4 (two runs, three input permutations, swap older/newer, `created_utc` the only difference), G-U1 for X versus X in S0, `test_unified_run_never_mutates_project_snapshot`, `test_cancel_keeps_previous_result`, `test_worker_output_is_validated_before_publish`, `test_worker_spawn_reuses_c3_argv_environment_and_memory_cap`, `test_children_get_absolute_argv_and_a_private_cwd`, `test_no_network_during_unified_run` (audit hook on socket events, in-process runs), `test_unified_import_dag` (AST; its checker is shown to fail on planted violations), `test_unified_has_no_qt` (fresh interpreter, PySide6 blocked), `test_cli_unified_run_exit_codes` (0/2/3/4/5), `test_cli_unified_show_json_single_document`, `test_cli_unified_worker_hidden_from_help`, `test_cli_unified_never_imports_qt`, banned-word and hostile-name sweeps, `test_unknown_source_id_renders_generically` (CLI level).
- Real child process: `test_a_real_child_process_produces_the_same_result_as_the_same_code_in_process` and `test_cli_unified_run_with_the_real_worker_child_process` run `python -P -m patchtrace unified _worker` through `jobs.run_process` (Linux: RLIMIT_AS by prlimit). Failure classification (cancel, timeout, exit 22, OOM text, Job Object peak, crash, signal, silent exit, error document, spawn failure) uses a stub runner, so what a Windows child reports at the memory limit (U7) is NOT verified here.
- Found while building: S0a `store._lines` did not catch `zlib.error` (a damaged byte inside a deflate stream escaped as an untyped exception); S0a-FIX fixed it and the owner-side mapping S0b had added was removed (02 s10 item 12). After merging S0a-FIX the store's `result-drift` check exposed a real gap in the S0b identity (a set refused for another pair and the three raw failure kinds changed the content but not the id): fixed, with `test_every_unusable_input_has_its_own_result_id_so_the_store_never_sees_drift`. `ProjectService.open` refuses a project whose raw output changed, so the partial-result paths for a changed or missing raw file are reachable only for an already open project (tested through the pipeline API). C3's `secondary --help` prints `==SUPPRESS==` for its hidden worker (not touched).
- Not verified here: Windows (CI) and Python 3.12; the frozen `PatchTrace.exe unified --help` and `unified _worker --self-check`; real Ghidriff/BinDiff/rz-diff data; whether real BinExport sets carry import vertices outside the image; MSVC output styles in the decompiler text (canon/1 is tested on imitated text).
- Run from a git worktree with `PYTHONPATH=src`: the editable install points at the main checkout, so subprocess-based tests (for example `tests/test_acceptance_smoke.py`) would otherwise import another tree. CI, with a clean checkout, is unaffected.
- Full gate once on 4802756: `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen PYTHONPATH=src python -m pytest -q` 2646 passed, 6 skipped (live BinDiff BLOCKED: not installed), 212 s; `python -m ruff check src tests tools` clean.
- After merging `work/a` (S0a-FIX, S2, PIL-K) and adapting S0b to it, full gate once on 675fa36: 4170 passed, 6 skipped (live BinDiff BLOCKED), 281 s; ruff clean. Four S0b tests had failed after the merge: three for the changed store contract (a refused set / raw failure kind changing content but not the id: fixed in the identity; U+FFFD instead of removal for hostile names; content limits only in `limits_digest`) and the import-cycle scan, which now counts import-time imports only (S2 imports under `TYPE_CHECKING`).
### [S1:extraction-ir] SPEC-31 S1 (2026-10-14, Linux container, CPython 3.13.16 and 3.12.3)
Toolchain for every live line: Ghidra 12.0.4 PUBLIC (archive SHA-256 `c3b458661d69e26e203d739c0c82d143cc8a4a29d9e571f099c2cf4bda62a120`, the digest the repository workflows pin), OpenJDK 21.0.12, pyghidra 3.0.2, JPype1 1.5.2, ghidriff 1.0.0 at the pinned commit `4f5d895`. Fetched into a scratch directory outside the repository, never committed. No target binary was executed.
| Check | Command / method | Result |
|---|---|---|
| S1 tests | 11 files (`tests/test_unified_{ir_operands,ir_graph,ir_pe_tables,ir_ingest,ir_reader,ir_records,extract_runner,retain_gzf,ghidra_script,packaging,live_tool}.py`) | 438 tests; 519 with the S0a unified files, also green on CPython 3.12.3 (the CI pin, scratch venv with pydantic 2.13.5) |
| Full gate | `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen python -m pytest -q`, once, in the foreground | 2806 passed, 6 skipped, 225 s on the final code commit (LIVE ACCEPTANCE BLOCKED: bindiff, as before) |
| Lint | `ruff check src tests tools` | only the pre-existing I001 in `tests/test_calibration_tools.py` (not S1) |
| Live, probe pair | `tools/live_unified_check.py` on clang/lld-link MSVC-ABI DLLs (7 functions per side, no `.pdata`) | L0-L5 all PASS: two fresh `analyze` runs give the same `content_digest`; `gzf` mode of the packed program gives the same digest and identical function records; no unlocated address operand; real Ghidriff with `retain_gzf` retained exactly two GZFs (`<artifact name>-<sha1[:6]>.gzf`), SHA-256 equal to the recorded value, and their `gzf` extraction gives the digest of the own analysis on both sides |
| Live, MinGW pair | same tool on `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc -shared -O0` builds of `tests/fixtures/{old,new}.c` (61 functions, 38 with `.pdata`) | all PASS; `pdata_confirmed` 38; extraction 17.6 s, ingest 0.1 s (older side) |
| Live, large pair | same tool, MinGW DLLs of about 900 KB (4,060 functions, 174,388 / 174,516 instructions) | all PASS incl. L5: h0 / h3 / hm induce the same equivalence classes as Ghidra's own ExactBytes / ExactInstructions / ExactMnemonics hashers (4,047 / 3,831 / 3,810 classes older; 4,047 / 3,846 / 3,828 newer); `pdata_confirmed` 4,037; `mask_unlocated` 0; extraction 91.0 s / 93.8 s, ingest 8.9 s / 10.1 s |
| Live, MSVC CI pair (first run, 2026-10-14) | `windows` job of `live-unified.yml` on PR #49: `cl /O2 /GS-` builds of `tests/fixtures/{old,new}.c` (377 functions per side), real Ghidra 12.0.4 | L0-L4 PASS, including Ghidriff with `retain_gzf` and gzf == analyze; the original L5 FAILED for h3 and hm (Ghidra splits functions that differ only by a NOP, the stored keys skip NOPs by design), so L5 was redefined (A-27): export fidelity against Ghidra-style keys plus refinement of the stored keys; verified with real Ghidra locally on a synthetic NOP / INT3 DLL pair (69 functions, 16 with skipped instructions, which failed the original L5) and the MinGW pair (61 functions, 13); the MSVC re-run and the Linux job (L6 cross-OS digests) are CI evidence still to come. The "large pair" row above passed the original L5 only because no function in those builds differed by a skipped instruction alone |
| Ingest cost | `ingest` of the 27.7 MB extractor output (4,060 functions, 174,388 instructions) | 9.9 s, peak RSS 52 MiB (16 MiB above the import baseline); SQLite 12.6 MB |
| MD index parity | independent C++ transcription of `graph_util.h` arithmetic (`std::sort` + `std::accumulate`), 3,000 random graphs | 0 mismatches (bit-identical); builtin `sum()` differs from the plain loop in 303 of 500 graphs. NOT compared with the real `bindiff --md_index` (no pinned BinDiff here) |
| Mutation sanity check | 28 deliberate one-line mutations (hash choice, masks, extents, orientation, guards, retention, seam conditions, script helpers) each run against the focused tests | 27 killed; 1 equivalent (a count guard made redundant by the name-set check). One real gap found and closed by a new test: unreachable blocks in back-edge counting |
| Frozen probe (Linux, PyInstaller 6.22.3) | a one-module entry importing only `patchtrace.backend`, built with the repository hooks directory | the hook fires from `backend.py`'s lazy import; the data file lands at `_internal/patchtrace/unified/extract/ghidra_script.py` and is byte-identical; `importlib.resources.files(...).read_bytes()` works in the frozen process |
Found and fixed while building: `collect_data_files` collected nothing when a stale editable install shadowed the source tree (the hook now uses its own repository path); a sibling directory named `ghidra` next to the script would shadow Ghidra's Java package (the script now lives alone in `script/`); strict `.pdata` union equality confirmed only 17 of 38 MinGW primaries (alignment padding: tolerance rule, 38 of 38); a bare `[disp32]` / `[GS:0x30]` displacement was unlocated; `Ordinal_<n>` export placeholders were taken as aliases; raw bidi characters in regex literals were replaced by escapes; `.gitattributes` gaps closed (script LF so its hash is the same on every OS, notice bytes, fixtures).
Not verified here, only the `live-unified` workflow can: everything on Windows (JVM, Job Object, pyghidra and paths on `windows-latest`), MSVC-built fixtures (operand typing and RVA-form switches were seen on clang and MinGW code only), a real Ghidriff-written GZF on Windows through `retain_gzf`, `content_digest` equality across operating systems on the same DLL bytes, the Windows frozen smoke U1 (and U2 once S0b lands) and the Windows PyInstaller hook, the FID `lib` marker, and BinDiff `--md_index` parity. Scale beyond 4,060 functions per side (L-35: 60k) was not measured.

### [RG:release-gate] Release gate for prerelease publication (2026-10-14, local Linux worktree on fd6cdba, Python 3.13.16; synthetic data, a stub `gh` and git history only, no Actions run)
`tools/release_gate.py` plus the `release-gate` job of `.github/workflows/qa-windows.yml` (spec `windows-acceptance-and-milestone-builds.md` section 1.1): `release` now runs only for a `v*` tag or when the gate prints `publish=true`, which it does at the third counted merge to `main` since the latest published `v0.1.0-beta.*` prerelease, for `[release]` in the head message, with no earlier prerelease or an anchor off the first-parent history, and on any gate failure (fail open; the count and the reason go into the release notes). A commit whose whole message holds one of the five GitHub skip markers is not counted, a late line of a squash body included; the head always is. Evidence: `tests/test_release_gate.py` 99 tests (the rule, markers in subject and body, fail-open for every tool failure and usage error, `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` injection, a throwaway real git repository with a side-branch merge, and no-YAML-library checks of the job graph, permissions, outputs and the shell-injection boundary), 126 passed together with `tests/test_workflows.py` and `tests/test_milestone_notes.py`; `ruff check` clean; `actionlint` clean and the edited workflow parsed with PyYAML; 33 deliberate one-line mutations of the tool and 23 of the workflow all caught (two survivors found on the way were closed by new assertions); the real CLI replayed over the first-parent history of origin/main at `fd6cdba` with each of the 44 local `v0.1.0-beta.*` tags as the anchor gave the same count as an independent `git rev-list --grep` in all 44 (anchor beta.225 gives 1 of 3: the head counts although its own squash body carries a marker, and the commit before it is excluded by a marker in its subject). Not verified here, only GitHub can: the gate job on a real push (`gh api` with the job token, the output hand-over to `release`, a skipped gate on `pull_request` skipping `release`, the notes line), the Windows run of the new tests, and the real release list (a stub `gh` stood in for it). Do not write the literal skip markers or `[release]` in commit messages or PR bodies unless intended: a squash body that quotes a skip marker skips the whole push run (PR #48), and `[release]` publishes.

Found while building, both checked on the existing `parse_exports`: a hypothesis case showed its plausibility rule (more names than addresses) flags a valid alias table (status `partial`, `export_count_implausible`), so the new parser bounds both counts by what the file could hold instead; RVA 0 lies in the header region, so a null name pointer is listed as the export `MZ` with status `ok` and no warning, while the new parser reads RVA 0 as nothing (which also stops a null address table from producing exports out of header bytes).

### [WBQ-S10:symbols-decoration] WBQ S10 symbol decoration (2026-10-14, Linux container, CPython 3.13.16; known answers also on 3.12.3 and 3.11.17)
The "decoder" below is pydemumble 0.1.3 (LLVM's MicrosoftDemangle.cpp) in a scratch virtual environment, never a repository dependency. The licence check is recorded in `tests/fixtures/wbq/ms_kat.NOTICE`.
| Check | Command / method | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Focused tests | `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen PYTHONPATH=src python -m pytest tests/test_symbols_decoration.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` | 180 passed, 1 skipped (the oracle test skips without pydemumble) |
| Same, decoder on the path | `PYTHONPATH=src:<scratch venv site-packages>` | 181 passed: 59 of 59 `demangled` cells identical, 32 of 32 name parts decode alone to `int <qualified_name>` |
| Lint | `ruff check src/patchtrace/symbols tests/test_symbols_decoration.py` | clean |
| Corpus differential | parser against decoder on all 1,083 distinct names of the 18 `ms-*.test` files | 371 derived, 337 complex, 375 not derived (string literals, RTTI), 0 disagreements |
| Mutation differential | 300,000 random 1-4 character edits of those names (seed 20261014); a derived name part must decode alone to `int <qualified_name>` | 75,885 derived, 0 disagreements |
| Chain differential | 100,000 generated scope chains (random digit back references, duplicates, `??0`/`??1`) | 24,362 derived, all rendered identically by the decoder |
| Chain differential, both directions | 150,000 generated chains with a valid type encoding | decoder and parser accept exactly the same names: 37,838 both accept with the same rendering, 112,162 both reject |
| Defect sanity check | 18 deliberate one-line defects applied to a copy of the package | 18 killed (the rule that a truncated `?` scope is complex first survived and is now pinned by two cases) |
| Interpreters | the 59 known answers through a bare harness on CPython 3.12.3 (the CI pin) and 3.11.17 | 59 of 59, 0 mismatches |
Found while building: a 510-character name can repeat a 252-character scope 252 times through digit back references (about 65,000 characters), so derived names are capped at 1,024 characters before the join (`test_back_references_cannot_expand_a_name_past_the_cap`). Real names end in `@<digit>` (`??_R3Base@@8`, `?foo@@9`, `??_B?1??getS@@YAAAUS@@XZ@51`), so the trailing `@N` byte count is stripped only after a character other than `@`. The 10-entry name table cap cannot be seen through the digits 0-9 (an equivalent mutant); it only bounds the work.
Not verified here: Windows, the Linux-versus-Windows decoder equality on the known answers (S11), the frozen build (nothing imports the package yet) and any real MSVC export table (the names are the LLVM test names and synthetic ones).

- Scale (shared container, 100,000 modified pairs of one callee change each, before this note): `GhidriffRows.resolve` 3.2 s, `GhidriffPairing` 4.0 s, `CandidateIndex` 0.9 s, `GhidriffCalls` 9.0 s (100,000 observations at about 70 us each, most of it `keys.make_observation`); an unchanged callee list is skipped without cleaning. - Not verified here: Windows (path handling of unified result directories, the child process of the purity test, CRLF), the frozen executable (nothing imports `pilot.sources` yet), real Ghidriff output (callee label spelling decides whether the PE witness ever applies), real capa/FLOSS/DiE runs, a unified result with ProgramIR.

### [FAB-0:fabric-core] Anchor fabric pure core (2026-10-14, builder worktree on fd6cdba; Linux container, synthetic data only)
- Red first: all six new test files failed at collection (`No module named 'patchtrace.fabric'`) before the package existed. Green: `tests/test_fabric_{contracts,ids,origins,memory,synergy,plan}.py` 331 passed in about 2 s on Python 3.13.16, and on Python 3.12.3 (scratch venv, pydantic 2.13.5, the CI pin) together with `tests/test_pilot_keys.py`; `ruff check` clean on `src/patchtrace/fabric` and the new tests.
- Gates: the identity formulas against an independent re-implementation of the domain-id formula plus pinned literal goldens; `pair_key` equal to `unified.keys.row_id` and `pilot.keys.candidate_key`; `components` equal to `pilot.origins.components` on random tag sets and the default table a superset of PIL's; `clean_line` equal to `sanitize_text`, code point by code point over the BMP; the plan's kind table, roles, vocabularies, error codes and limits equal to the code (`test_fabric_plan.py`); the synergy scenario of plan s5; order independence of publishes (all 24 orders of 4 batches), idempotent republish, atomic failure, replacement per (producer, batch), concurrent writers.
- Test strength: 71 single-point mutations of a scratch copy of `src` (identity payloads, key folding and grammar, role and side rules, orientation reasons, origin guard, replacement, merge, limits, atomicity, locking, strictness, vocabularies, exit codes). First run 65 killed and 4 survived: tie-breaking by subject was exercised by too few rows, the writer lock was not exercised, `extra='forbid'` was tested through a payload that failed for another reason, and the `Resolution` status check was untested. Each is now closed by a test (an 8-row tie test, a deterministic writer-gating test, JSON-mode strictness cases, a status test); second run 71 of 71 killed.
- Measured at F-01 (20,000 one-witness anchors with facets, CPython 3.13.16, one machine): building 2.26 s, `publish` 0.52 s, a 500-row `find` page 71 ms, `resolve` 14 ms, `digest` 0.07 s, `verify_anchor` over all 0.74 s; the JSON is 11.1 MiB (583 bytes per anchor) and revalidates in 0.75 s. Budgets to re-measure on Windows CI.
- Found and fixed while building: the authoring tool turned typed `\u` escapes into the raw characters, which put 25 invisible characters (bidirectional controls, U+2028 and U+2029, a combining mark) into the sources; they are now built from code points and `test_no_fabric_file_hides_an_invisible_character` scans every FAB file; `api_key` is not idempotent (a second fold), so it is documented as applied once to a producer's raw name; the plan's first draft claimed a 20,000-anchor batch always fits the 32 MiB row, which only holds for typical one-witness anchors (the store, not F-01, must refuse a body above 24 MiB).
- Regression subset (existing tests that scan source trees or docs: unified packaging, import DAG and contracts, calibration runtime, PE overview, KBC contracts, backend bundle build, spec registry, pilot contracts and keys): 884 passed, 1 skipped (pre-existing). The full suite was not run (the integrator runs it twice). GitHub was not consulted (worker brief rule: no GitHub access).
- Not verified here: Windows (CRLF checkout, short ids, path handling) and CI; the frozen smoke (nothing imports `patchtrace.fabric` yet); behaviour with the enrichment store, the GUI and the CLI (later slices); no live or Microsoft data was involved.

1. View > Theme > System, then switch Windows between light and dark: window, tables, chips and code panes follow without restart; text stays readable.

### [RA-FIX-1:audit-fixes] Random audit 1 fixes RA-1, RA-4, RA-8, RA-9, RA-10 (2026-10-14, worktree on f94875c; Linux container, synthetic data only)
- Red first: each new test file failed before its fix (entropy budget and layout 9 of 10, offline cache 10 of 11, hostile text 3 failures after the predicate existed, bench RSS 4 of 4, `-0.0` 1 of 1), for the reason the audit named (for example 263,024 bytes read by `layout_from_reader` on a 128 KiB image; `0.0 is None` for the RSS report). Green: 53 new tests in 1.5 s (`tests/test_pe_entropy_budget.py` 11, `test_intel_offline_cache.py` 11, `test_hostile_text.py` 27, `test_bench_enrichment_report.py` 4); no test measures wall time.
- Recipe R1 at full size (96 sections over one 8 MiB range, 8,392,776 bytes): 34.3 s before (audit), 1.4 s after, status `partial`, warning `entropy_budget_exhausted`; the honest one-section file of the same size 0.5 s before and after. Recipe R4 and R6 are covered by scaled tests (`test_an_over_cap_legacy_index_survives_a_put_untouched`, `test_the_report_says_null_not_zero_without_the_resource_module`).
- Regression subset: two overlapping selections (67 and 34 test files) of the tests that touch `pe_overview`, `inspect_pe`, the tool adapters, `tier_w`, unified, preflight and the inspection UI (3378 and 856 passed; the skips and xfails are existing markers); `tests/test_intel_transport.py`, `test_intel_cli.py`, `test_intel_discovery.py` and `test_enrichment_perf.py` pass; `ruff check` clean on every file changed. The full suite was not run (the integrator runs it twice).
- Test strength: four one-line mutations (budget off by one, budget never spent, `put` reading its shard leniently, format characters dropped from the hostile rule) each failed 3 to 11 of the new tests.
- Cost of the budget: a file whose sections and overlay hold more than 32 MiB of raw data (each range counts at most 8 MiB) now reports `entropy` None, or a shorter sample, for the ranges after that and status `partial`; typical Windows binaries (one or two sections above 8 MiB) are unchanged.
- Not verified here: Windows (shard file names, `os.replace` over an existing shard, the macOS `ru_maxrss` unit is tested with a stand-in module only), real Microsoft binaries, a cache written by the first version on a user's machine (only a synthetic `urls.json`), more than one process writing one cache.

- Red first: `tests/test_network_consent_guard.py` failed at collection (`No module named 'network_guard'`) before the guard existed. The credential scan was red before the `redact_log` change: `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=` and a fine-grained `github_pat_` token reached `analysis.log` and the UI log event of a real fake-child run. The survivor removes the CSAF host-consent check, an equivalent mutant while the URL allowlist names the only consentable host. Six cases were added after survivors (connect and sendto by host name, `Authorization: Token`, a bare `bearer`, the exit hint, the acquisition allowlist).
- Findings: the default `restrict_env=False` child inherits proxy and CA variables and tokens whatever the symbol setting (only restricted and bundle runs follow SPEC-42 row 9); `redact_log` leaves Cookie, SAS, Slack, JWT and `--password value` shapes; `cli.main` echoes unexpected exception text unredacted.

### [PIL-B2:engine] Patch-intent localization fusion, tiers, ranking and the service (2026-10-14, builder worktree on 994c336 (work/a); Linux container, CPython 3.13.16 and 3.12.3, synthetic data only)
- Focused run: `tests/test_pilot_{fusion,ranking,properties,synergy,sample_e2e}.py` 175 passed (fusion 48, ranking 50, properties 15, the PIL-T oracle 62 of which 22 were `xfail(strict)` and now pass for real) on both interpreters (3.12.3 in a throwaway venv without Qt); all ten `tests/test_pilot_*.py` files together 1128 passed in 46 s; `ruff check src/patchtrace/pilot tests/test_pilot_*.py tests/pilot_*.py` clean. The full suite was not run (no existing source file changed). `ruff check src tests tools` reports 4 import-order findings in four files this slice did not touch (`test_calibration_tools`, `test_site_build`, `test_unified_ladder_core`, `tools/build_windows.py`).
- Oracle: only the 11 `@PIL_B2` decorators and 2 imports were removed and two docstring sentences refreshed; no assertion changed; `tests/pilot_synergy_support.py` is untouched (its `localize()` seam works because `LocalizeInputs` is structural). The T track's note that a reference engine passes all 22 is confirmed independently by this implementation.
- Invariants, each with a test that fails when the rule is broken: shared origin counts once (advisory repeated 1 to 4 times, B1 plus ChangeFacts plus call label, never summed); no float, division or rounding in the six modules (AST scan) and an integer-tuple order key; the tier derivation over all 15,360 fact combinations returns only assignable mechanisms (never `strong`) and is monotone in every flag; removing shape evidence or adding doubt never raises a class on random scenarios; a change is "not seen" only where every channel it reads is complete and the candidate is a one-to-one pair; ties are the equal-key groups and the ranks tile 1..n; canonical JSON round trip and `verify_assessment`; the result is byte-identical across `PYTHONHASHSEED` 1 and 2; cancel before and during a run, the deadline, P-01 and P-03; the P-04 cut (700 partial and 300 queue candidates give 500 + 200 leads, true counts 700 + 300).
- Mutation check (throwaway script `mutate.py`, mutate one source line, run the five test files with `-x`, restore): 18 single-point mutations: toolchain cap removed, window cap removed, label-only firmness removed, the two-code-origins rule removed, one-sided candidates compared, tie order by input order, every advisory entry its own origin, a proof no longer forces review, `-met` sign flipped, unusable channels read, locality threshold `>=` to `>`, duplicates not collapsed, queue cut off by one, the advisory entry no longer protected from the P-02 cut, an unusable pairing channel not abstaining, counts taken from the kept leads, the advisory listed for a lead it does not back: 17 killed (the P-02 one only after its test was made deterministic by choosing the entry that sorts first); the survivor (`window.state != 'no'` for `== 'yes'`) is equivalent because an `unknown` window lists no CVEs and the CVE-count test follows it.
- Found while building: (1) a P-02 cut made the result `partial` with no limitation line, which the contract refuses; the status is now derived from the limitation lines so the two cannot disagree. (2) The plan's fixed header "Provenance" contains the stem of the banned word "proven"; the sweep follows the repo's convention (stems as prefixes, the rest as whole words). (3) An observation of a channel that did not run is not read, so a test that expects a build fact must make the Build channel `available` (the helper does).
- Scale (this container, CPU time): 5,000 pairs x 8 CVEs, half of the pairs with evidence: 1.2 s; every pair with four observations and two hypotheses per CVE: 2.2 s (plan s7 target 5 s); before the pattern-shared evaluation the heavy case took 3.9 s.
- Not verified here: Windows (the hash-seed test spawns Python; paths are not used), real Ghidriff/capa/FLOSS/DiE/unified data, the frozen executable (nothing imports `pilot.service` yet), the 250,000-candidate limit and the 1 GiB bound (P-01 and P-08 are exercised at 3 candidates and by design: only keys are kept per candidate and context, the kept leads are rebuilt), multi-origin observations (none exist; the connected-component calculus is not monotone under removal for them).

Before the equal-`h1` positional alignment 12 untouched switch functions (twin case bodies) were `review` too |

R-01..R-04, R-07, R-16: 13 classes x 20 random members + the named cases (`tests/test_unified_gu_mutants.py`) | leakage into `cosmetic` / `unchanged` = 0; the planted class is among the deltas in every member; frame-only and trace-arg members are `review` |

Found and fixed while building: equal masked bytes at a moved RVA say nothing about where a relative operand points (R-03), so UNCH/1 needs the target consistency of every address operand; a function must not prove its own data references (consensus needs two voters, region shift needs nobody else to have voted); the identical-artifact shortcut and the PE-header rule were needed for X versus X to be 100 % `unchanged` on real output; thunk collapse had to run before the exclusive-partner conflict; switch successors needed the CFG-position pairing; bodies without instructions (a lone jump) pair by the key `None`; equal-`h1` pairs align positionally (the first rule of 05 s2.3) because switch cases with equal bodies have no unique key.
Not verified here: Windows and MSVC-built IR (RVA-form switches, CFG dispatch, /GS), Python 3.12 (CI pin), the frozen executable; no end-to-end `unified run` with IR (`compute.py` does not pass `ir_sides` yet: integration owner); G-U1 P0 / P1 and G-U2 recall at >= 20 mutants per class on the real Tier R corpus, G-U6 and G-U7 (S6); BinDiff parity; the 6 CRT start-up functions of both live pairs show that single-use data without a confirmed neighbour stays `unmapped` (a candidate extra DATA-MAP rule is listed in tasks/todo.md [S3:ir-facts]).

### [FAB-1:fabric-store] Anchor fabric enrichment store and schemas (2026-10-14, builder worktree on the FAB-0 merge; Linux container, synthetic data only)
- Red first: `tests/test_fabric_store.py` failed at collection (`No module named 'patchtrace.fabric.store'`) and `tests/test_fabric_schemas.py` for the missing `docs/schemas/fabric/`. Green: the eight fabric test files 440 passed on CPython 3.13.16 and on 3.12.3 (shared scratch venv, pydantic 2.13.5): store 52, schemas 57, the FAB-0 files 331 (one hygiene test extended); `ruff check` clean on `src/patchtrace/fabric`, the new tests and `tools/generate_fabric_schemas.py`.
- Gates: a second store reads back what the first published (digest, `find`, `get`, `batches`); every step of a scripted sequence and 25 random sequences of publish and retract (hypothesis) give the reports, digests, `find` pages and `resolve` answers of `MemoryAnchorStore`, and a fresh reader over the stored rows gives the reference digest after each step; the batch digest equals an independent oracle of the domain-id formula and a pinned golden; a moved origin table and a row stored with a moved guard are listed stale with their anchors still read; after a real `swap_orientation()` the old orientation's anchors are skipped and counted, statements stay, and swapping the snapshot back restores everything; rows are untouched by `apply_retention`, `prune_runs` and `make_room`; an old reader (`KINDS` without the kind) sees the same secondary data, keeps the rows, `extension_version` stays 1 and the project saves and loads; every invalid example is rejected for its own message.
- Test strength: 65 single-point mutations (key, guard, project and row checks, read verification, orientation, stale rules, merge limit, row cutting and ordering, row cap boundary and escaping, retract kind filter and read-only, cache signature parts, snapshot cache, error mapping, status fields, the model validators and strictness). First run 59 killed; 6 survivors: 4 test gaps (a refused retract on a read-only store served the mutated view, rows of a project that was never saved, an unusable project id, an unusable artifact pair), each now closed by a test, and 2 equivalent mutants (a duplicated guard and a duplicated key argument), whose redundant code was removed; 65 of 65 killed after.
- Measured at F-01 (20,000 one-witness anchors with facets, CPython 3.13.16, one shared machine, load about 4): `publish` 1.5 s (reference alone 0.7 s), the row 10.2 MiB (535 bytes per anchor), first read of a fresh store (parse, validate, `verify_anchor`, merge) 1.8 s, a read with nothing changed 28 ms, a 500-row `find` page 25 ms, `resolve` 8 ms, `digest` 47 ms, `retract` with its load 2.5 s. Budgets to re-measure on Windows CI.
- Found while building: the plan's guard fields `run_id` and `raw_sha256` were not defined, so they are the fixed `fabric` and empty (a new Ghidriff run does not stale a batch about two images); `EnrichmentStore.publish` commits per row, so a call touching several batches cannot be one transaction (documented, repeat completes); a cached view must be dropped after a refused write because the database signature does not move (mutation survivor, now tested); the project snapshot can be megabytes, so it is parsed once per snapshot hash, not per read.
- Regression subset (storage, retention, kinds, hardening, intelligence storage, unified store, pilot contracts and keys, spec registry, unified import DAG and packaging, KBC contracts): 752 passed. The full suite was not run (the integrator runs it twice). GitHub was not consulted (worker brief rule).
- Not verified here: Windows and CI; the frozen smoke (nothing imports `patchtrace.fabric.store` yet); worker-thread use (the store is owner-thread only, checked by a test); a real producer; the GUI and CLI slices.

- Gates: pins survive a batch replacement that drops the anchor, the retraction of the batch, a retraction by the `analyst` producer and `apply_retention`, `prune_runs` and `make_room`, with every pin row byte-identical afterwards, and match the anchor again when a batch brings it back; the sequence decides when the clock ties and the row ids fall; a random sequence of 14 pin and unpin steps against a plain model (25 examples), also read back by a fresh store; 8 kinds of unreadable event are counted and never hide the good pins; a pin made before a swap is listed stale, an anchor of the other orientation is refused, and a stale pin is rewritten when pinned again; notes at and over 2 KiB (ASCII, two-byte, lines); an old reader keeps the row, `extension_version` stays 1 and the project saves and loads; the event digest equals an independent hand-built hash and a pinned golden.
- Test strength: 42 single-point mutations of `pins.py` (kind, caps and boundaries, model validators, note cleaning, idempotence and refresh, sequence and ordering, stale rules, unpin, event reading and counting, input checks, time zone, guard fields, pair at pin time, unsaved project, domain). First run 39 killed; the 3 survivors were test gaps (the `via` of an unpin event, and the two unsaved-project branches when rows exist but no snapshot); each is now tested; 42 of 42 killed. After extracting the shared project access from `store.py`, the 65 store mutations were run again and all are killed.
- Measured (CPython 3.13.16, shared machine): 1,000 pins one after another 16.5 s (16.5 ms each, each reading all earlier events), `pins()` over 1,000 events 27 ms, one more pin over 1,000 events 49 ms.
- Found while building: a pin made while the files were compared the other way round names the wrong image side, so the event stores the project's two hashes and the pin is listed stale after a swap; re-pinning a stale pin with the same note must still write an event (mutation survivor, now tested); `Field(max_length)` placed after an `AfterValidator` reports `too_long`, not `string_too_long`.
- Not verified here: Windows and CI; the frozen smoke (nothing imports `patchtrace.fabric.pins`); WB1's button and the GUI; worker-thread use (owner thread only, checked by a test).
### [R1:rules-synth] YRX R1 closed-grammar synthesis (2026-10-14, Linux container 4 vCPU, Python 3.13.16, yara-x 1.21.0 from the wheel, hypothesis 6.169; SYNTHETIC images only, none of it live evidence)
| Check | Method | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Tests | `pytest tests/test_rules_synth.py` (pure and engine) | 333 passed in about 28 s, three consecutive runs identical; also 333 passed on CPython 3.12.3 (the CI interpreter) in a scratch venv with pydantic 2.14.1 in 36 s; the 422 R0 and R2 tests still pass; `ruff check` clean on the two files |
| Hostile values, no engine | a lexical oracle blanks every string literal of a rule (closed alphabet, no line break inside, nothing outside printable ASCII and LF) and compares the rest with the same rule built from benign values; 54 named hostile values (quotes, backslashes, escaped quotes, NUL, LF, CR, tab, DEL, C1 controls, star-slash, slash-star, braces, a whole second rule, keywords, `import "pe"`, `include`, pattern references, truncated escapes, bidi and zero-width marks, line separators, an emoji, lone surrogates) and 200 hypothesis examples, each in every value position at once (label, project id, function names, CVE, KB, CWE, capability, import and DLL names, pin names, text-pattern bytes) | skeleton identical every time |
| Hostile values, real YARA-X | the same values compiled under R2's policy, read back through the engine (meta keys and values, tags, pattern ids, imports), formatted twice; text-pattern bytes scanned inside PE images in all three encodings, with a flipped byte as the control; header lines of any content under `with_header` | 0 errors, 0 warnings, values read back exactly (non-ASCII as `?`), every text a `Formatter` fixed point, exact bytes found and the control not |
| Exhaustive | every byte value alone and all 256 together as a text pattern; every ASCII character in one meta value | the engine decodes each to the bytes that went in |
| Caps | one rule with every value at its longest, every character four characters long in the source (60,171 bytes, 65 meta entries, a 512-byte pattern, four pins, five patterns, eight gates) | compiles clean and is a fixed point; a value past its cap, or a rule past 64 KiB, is a `PT-RULE-LIMIT` (refusal table) |
| Behaviour on images | call pins on a two-call function in a second code section (right slots, swapped, one wrong, same slot, shifted by padding, two copies satisfying one pin each, the 8th and 9th match), named, ordinal and delay-load imports (DLL case, absent, wrong table), the PE, AMD64 and executable-section gates, text patterns in ASCII and UTF-16 | all as designed; the masked bytes alone hit every variant, the pins separate them |
| Timing | best of 5 runs | `render_rule` 0.3 ms for a 2.5 KB rule (features included) and 5.5 ms for the 60 KB rule; `engine.lint` (compile, build, Formatter check) 16 ms and 23 ms; the Formatter alone 10 ms for 60 KB |
| Mutation sanity | 61 one-line mutations of `synth.py` (quote and backslash escaping, controls and DEL raw, non-ASCII passed through, exact quoting made lossy, comment keeping LF, `fullmatch` to `match`, unvalidated lineage or direction, every pin arithmetic term, the match bound, scope, machine and PE gates, duplicate and order handling, advisory order and cap, side, canonical text and encoding checks, alphabet widened, size caps, raw interpolation of project id, label, capability and function names) | 56 of the first 57 killed on the first run; the survivor (function names on by default) was a real test gap and got a test; the four mutations added for the type-versus-cap refinements were killed at once; 61 of 61 killed after. The engine-free skeleton tests alone kill the escaping and interpolation mutations. The module was prototyped against the engine before its tests existed, so this check stands in for red-first |
| Deep runs | the same tests with 5,000 examples per pure property and 1,000 per engine property (5,000 for the header lines), hypothesis seeds 11 and 12 on the code before the last refinements and 21 and 22 on the final code | pure 220 passed in 110 s and 87 s, engine 105 passed in 316 s and 284 s; no failure |
Found while building: YARA-X 1.21.0 has no `pe.delayed_imports`; `pe.imports(dll, ordinal)` is an integer (the bare form warns `non_bool_expr`); `pe.imports` compares function names without regard to case; an unused declared pattern is E022; `allowed_metadata` does not reject unlisted meta keys; the Formatter re-indents continuation lines and aligns `=` per section; an empty text pattern is E024 and one-byte or all-0x00/0x90/0xFF patterns are E018 (slow pattern). The editing tool of this session turns a typed backslash-u escape into the literal character (the same slip as in PIL-K1/K2), so bidirectional and zero-width characters in the tests are built with `chr()`.
Not verified here: Windows (CI; the goldens need LF checkouts), the frozen build, real third-party PEs and real masks (R4), classic YARA (the rules target YARA-X), and the full suite (the integrator runs it).

### [REDACT-2:redaction-coverage] redact_log coverage and linear time (2026-10-14, worker A40 on the CONSENT base d793ac3; Linux container; CPython 3.13.16; synthetic data only)
- Red first (`tests/test_redaction_coverage.py` against the unchanged code): 39 of the 53 non-timing tests failed (every Cookie, SAS, Slack, JWT, argv and generated-secret case and the CLI cases); the 14 that passed are the prose, digest, idempotence and URL-regression guards. Both timing tests failed: the 1 MiB corpus test was killed at its 180 s limit on its first input (`'a' * 1048576`), the hypothesis mixtures test failed at 64 KiB (2.8 s against 2 s). Cause, found by that test: the existing `_URL_AUTH` rule was quadratic (`redact_log('a' * N)`: 10k 0.69 s, 20k 3.3 s, 40k 15 s; hours for 1 MiB), and `redact_log` runs over child logs of up to 2 MiB (`backend.py:606`), so an unlucky or hostile log could stall the analysis worker.
- Green: the new file 57 passed in about 10 s (6.5 s of it the 1 MiB corpus, 36 inputs timed in one child, each about 0.2 s against an 8 s budget); `ruff check` clean on it, `diagnostics.py` and `cli.py`. Existing tests that import `diagnostics` or run `cli.main`: 677 passed, 4 skipped (the skips are existing) over 32 files in one run, and 17 further Qt, CLI and engine files (`test_ui`, `test_ui_engine_setup*`, `test_acceptance_smoke`, `test_nway_*`, the rules and kbc files, ...) pass one process per file. Stacking those 17 into a single process hit `MemoryError` in Qt fixtures; each file passes alone, and the batch did not contain the new file.
- Benign text is unchanged and not slower: output identical to the old function on log-like text, 0.197 s per MiB against 0.218 s (the literal-anchored `://` rule is faster than the old one and pays for the five added passes).
- Exploration beyond the committed 60-example properties (scratch, nothing committed): 30,000 generated secrets per shape, none survived; idempotent on all 30,000 realistic line mixtures. Of 30,000 adversarial fragment mixtures 55 were not idempotent, all inside the old `_ASSIGNMENT` rule (a quoted value directly followed by more text, for example `token=''--token`), none involving the new rules; left as is.
- Mutation check (scratch copy outside the repository): 21 single-point mutants of `diagnostics.py` and `cli.py` (each new rule removed or weakened, the old quadratic rule restored, the CLI left raw on stderr or in JSON); 19 killed. The 2 survivors remove a lookbehind from the JWT or argv rule: the explicit caps already bound the damage (about 5.5 s against 0.19 s on `'eyJ' * N`, 2.7 s against 0.23 s on `'--' * N` per MiB), so they stay as a 12 to 29 times speed-up that is not test-pinned. Reviewing the JWT lookbehind during the mutation work showed that it would miss a token after an escaped delimiter (`%3DeyJ...`); the escape alternative and a `jwt-escaped` case were added, and a mutant without the alternative is killed. The marker guard of the argv rule is pinned both ways (removed, and loosened so that `[redacted]` with text glued to it leaks).
- Limits: a JWT segment longer than its cap (8 KiB, 64 KiB, 8 KiB) or a token that follows base64url text directly is not recognised; the Cookie rule hides the rest of the line, including JSON that follows on the same line; `-p x` and `curl -u user:pass` are not covered. Not verified here: Windows and CPython 3.12 (the tests avoid POSIX-only calls; possessive quantifiers need 3.11 or later and the project requires 3.12), the frozen executable, the full suite.
